\section{CNNA architectural identity: ComplemeNt Net
Architecture}\label{cnna-architectural-identity-complement-net-architecture}

CNNA means \textbf{ComplemeNt Net Architecture}. The name identifies the
invariant construction principle, not a late physical interpretation:
one growing provenance net is repeatedly resolved into a realized or
retained part, a relative complement, and an interface or handoff. The
role of the complement is stage-, cut-, scale-, and projection-relative.

The architecture begins before any Schur/DtN or quantum specialization.
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C001}} supplies the empty baseline; \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}} creates the
first realized node; \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C003}} and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C018}} expose
not-yet-realized continuation slots; \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C004A}} selects the first
such slot; and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C013}}/\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C014}} produce the first nontrivial
response-capable directed net. Later complement elimination, record/live
splitting, nested refinements, and reduced observations preserve this
throughline in different mathematical forms.

This architectural statement is interpretive and organizational. It does
not add a theorem beyond the registered node contracts.

\section{Conceptual scope: generalized open provenance
systems}\label{conceptual-scope-generalized-open-provenance-systems}

The general research object is an \textbf{open provenance system}: an
observed present is treated as a reduced view of a real, historically
grown provenance state. The current CNNA DAG is one finite deterministic
specialization, not a proof that every empirical open system has this
form.

A generic state is organized schematically as

\[
\mathfrak P_n=(\Gamma_n,\mathsf{Rec}_n,\mathsf{Live}_n,\mathcal K_n,\mathcal C_n,\mathcal O_n).
\]

The intended roles are: event provenance, immutable birth record,
mutable live response, response kernel, cut-relative complement, and
observation/coarse-graining channel. The weak hypothesis allows
intrinsic stochasticity after provenance completion. The strong CNNA
hypothesis seeks deterministic completion. \textbf{The strong universal
claim is not currently proved.}

\subsection{Non-identifications}\label{non-identifications}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Schur/DtN elimination is an effective response reduction, not a
  partial trace.
\item
  A POVM supplies outcome probabilities; an instrument additionally
  supplies outcome-conditioned state updates.
\item
  A process tensor or quantum comb is a quantum multi-time
  specialization, not the definition of provenance itself.
\item
  Event provenance may remain acyclic even when the live state has
  recurrent or cyclic dynamics.
\end{itemize}

The reduced-dynamics ambiguity under initial correlations motivates a
provenance-dependent reconstruction map.
\textbf{EXT-REF-OPS-PECHUKAS-001 --- specialization context.} Philip
Pechukas, \emph{Reduced Dynamics Need Not Be Completely Positive},
Physical Review Letters 73(8) (1994), 1060--1062. DOI:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{10.1103/PhysRevLett.73.1060}}. Exact location: principal result.
Context: Initial correlations and assignment-map qualification of
reduced dynamics. Formal status:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{INTERPRETIVE\_SPECIALIZATION\_CONTEXT\_NOT\_IMPORTED\_AS\_AXIOM}}

The process-tensor framework is the primary quantum reference for
operational multi-time memory. \textbf{EXT-REF-OPS-POLLOCK-001 ---
specialization context.} Felix A. Pollock, César Rodríguez-Rosario,
Thomas Frauenheim, Mauro Paternostro, and Kavan Modi,
\emph{Non-Markovian quantum processes: Complete framework and efficient
characterization}, Physical Review A 97(1) (2018), 012127. DOI:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{10.1103/PhysRevA.97.012127}}. Exact location: Abstract and
process-tensor construction. Context: Operational multi-time memory and
process statistics. Formal status:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{INTERPRETIVE\_SPECIALIZATION\_CONTEXT\_NOT\_IMPORTED\_AS\_AXIOM}}

The Davies--Lewis instrument framework is the target distinction between
probabilities and post-measurement states.
\textbf{EXT-REF-OPS-DAVIESLEWIS-001 --- specialization context.} E. B.
Davies and J. T. Lewis, \emph{An operational approach to quantum
probability}, Communications in Mathematical Physics 17(3) (1970),
239--260. DOI: \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{10.1007/BF01647093}}. Exact location: operational
instrument framework. Context: Outcome probabilities and
outcome-conditioned state updates. Formal status:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{INTERPRETIVE\_SPECIALIZATION\_CONTEXT\_NOT\_IMPORTED\_AS\_AXIOM}}

The quantum-network/comb formalism is retained as context for later
compositional specialization. \textbf{EXT-REF-OPS-CHIRIBELLA-001 ---
specialization context.} Giulio Chiribella, Giacomo Mauro D'Ariano, and
Paolo Perinotti, \emph{Theoretical framework for quantum networks},
Physical Review A 80(2) (2009), 022339. DOI:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{10.1103/PhysRevA.80.022339}}. Exact location: quantum-network and
comb framework. Context: Compositional multi-step quantum
specialization. Formal status:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{INTERPRETIVE\_SPECIALIZATION\_CONTEXT\_NOT\_IMPORTED\_AS\_AXIOM}}

\subsection{Legacy POVM migration
guard}\label{legacy-povm-migration-guard}

The retained Legacy calculation constructed a finite Parseval-frame POVM
from a compressed response state, \[
E_v=z_vz_v^{\mathsf T},\qquad \sum_vE_v=I,\qquad p_v=\operatorname{tr}(D_NE_v).
\] It is evidence and a migration specification. It did \textbf{not}
establish exact projective compatibility of independently reconstructed
depths, an infinite state limit, a lift to the raw Weyl net, or a full
outcome-conditioned instrument. Any new DAG nodes for this chain must be
derived from current predecessors rather than importing Legacy
coefficients or numerical fallback rules.

\subsection{General completion
hypothesis}\label{general-completion-hypothesis}

For an empirically accessible open system \(S\), the research program
asks whether there exist a provenance-complete extension \(\widehat S\),
a retained region \(B\), a complement-elimination operation
\(\operatorname{Eff}_{I_B}\), and an observation channel \(Q_B\) such
that the complete family of observable process statistics satisfies

\[
\mathbb P_S
=
\mathbb P_{Q_B\circ\operatorname{Eff}_{I_B}(\widehat S)}.
\]

The nontrivial content is not merely that the system has a past. It is
that the dynamically sufficient part of that past remains a real
component of the present state and can alter future responses even when
two reduced present descriptions coincide.

Let \(X_n\) denote a reduced present and \(\Pi_n\) its dynamically
relevant provenance. The target sufficient state is

\[
Z_n=(X_n,\Pi_n),
\qquad
\mathbb P(X_{n+1}\mid X_{\le n},a_{\le n})
=
\mathbb P(X_{n+1}\mid Z_n,a_n).
\]

This equation defines a target property. The present DAG establishes it
only for those finite deterministic update components that are
explicitly formalized; it does not establish the quantifier over all
empirical systems.

\subsubsection{Weak and strong forms}\label{weak-and-strong-forms}

The \textbf{weak completion hypothesis} allows an intrinsically
stochastic update kernel

\[
Z_{n+1}\sim\mathcal U_n(\,\cdot\mid Z_n,a_n).
\]

It claims only that omitted history is no longer an additional source of
non-Markovianity once the sufficient provenance state is included.

The \textbf{strong deterministic CNNA hypothesis} instead seeks

\[
Z_{n+1}=\mathcal U_n(Z_n,a_n),
\]

or \(Z_{n+1}=\mathcal U_n(Z_n)\) in the absence of an external
intervention. This strong form is an ontological hypothesis over and
above the current finite proofs.

\subsection{Cut-relative environment and coherent
refinement}\label{cut-relative-environment-and-coherent-refinement}

For one complete finite carrier \(V_n\) and retained region
\(B_n\subseteq V_n\), define

\[
I_{B_n}=V_n\setminus B_n.
\]

The environment is therefore a role relative to a cut. If
\(B_1\subseteq B_2\), then \(I_{B_2}\subseteq I_{B_1}\): a coordinate
treated as environment at one resolution may be retained at a finer
resolution.

A generalized open-provenance theory must consequently prove more than
the existence of an effective response at each isolated cut. For nested
retained regions \(B_1\subseteq B_2\subseteq B_3\), the corresponding
reductions should satisfy a compositional law whenever all domains are
admissible,

\[
\operatorname{Eff}_{B_3\setminus B_1}
=
\operatorname{Eff}_{B_2\setminus B_1}
\circ
\operatorname{Eff}_{B_3\setminus B_2},
\]

or else expose the controlled defect as a refinement cocycle. C044,
C045, C047, C046, and C043 are the designated DAG locus for this
requirement.

\subsection{Record/live divergence and
backreaction}\label{recordlive-divergence-and-backreaction}

The immutable record and mutable live state have different mathematical
roles:

\[
\mathsf{Rec}_{n+1}=\mathsf{Rec}_n\cup\{r_{n+1}\},
\qquad
\mathsf{Live}_{n+1}=\mathcal U_n(\mathsf{Live}_n,r_{n+1},\Lambda_n).
\]

A record stores the conditions under which a relation was born. The live
channel stores how that relation currently acts after later growth.
Their difference is not treated as bookkeeping noise; it is the
prospective carrier of backreaction and effective memory. C008 owns the
update, C016 the immutable record, C017 the live channel, C024 the
backreaction stream, and C043 its multiscale reconstruction.

\subsection{Non-Markovianity and
identifiability}\label{non-markovianity-and-identifiability}

In this framework Markovianity is relative to the chosen state
description. A reduced state can be non-Markovian because two provenance
states \(\Pi_n^{(1)}\ne\Pi_n^{(2)}\) yield the same \(X_n\) but
different future response laws. This motivates, but does not prove, the
interpretation

\[
\text{effective memory}
=
\text{dynamically visible unresolved provenance}.
\]

The corresponding inverse problem is identifiability. Provenances
\(\Pi_1\) and \(\Pi_2\) are observationally equivalent at a chosen
intervention class if they induce the same complete family of process
statistics. The scientifically meaningful reconstruction target may
therefore be a provenance-equivalence class rather than a unique
microscopic history.

\subsection{Specialization ladder}\label{specialization-ladder}

The intended order is one-way and derived-only:

\[
\begin{aligned}
&\text{event provenance and response-coupled growth}\\
&\quad\longrightarrow\text{cut-relative effective response}\\
&\quad\longrightarrow\text{record/live and nested-channel coherence}\\
&\quad\longrightarrow\text{response algebra and positive state structures}\\
&\quad\longrightarrow\text{finite POVM and quantum-process specializations}\\
&\quad\longrightarrow\text{Weyl/GNS/Araki--Woods and modular/AQFT structures}.
\end{aligned}
\]

The reverse interpretation is not licensed: provenance is not defined
retrospectively from a quantum state, and a quantum reduction is not
assumed in order to derive the earlier response structure.

\subsection{Falsification and obstruction
gates}\label{falsification-and-obstruction-gates}

The generalized program must be weakened or rejected for a proposed
system class if any of the following persists after all admissible
extensions are considered:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  no finite or controlled provenance-sufficient state reproduces the
  observable multi-time statistics;
\item
  effective reductions for nested cuts cannot be made coherent and no
  controlled cocycle accounts for their defect;
\item
  record/live separation fails to define invariant birth data and a
  well-defined current state;
\item
  two supposedly equivalent provenance representatives yield different
  physical outputs after the registered quotient;
\item
  the finite POVM migration cannot derive positivity and completeness
  from current predecessors without importing Legacy fallback choices;
\item
  the OQS/AQFT specialization requires independent physical assumptions
  that are falsely presented as consequences of primitive provenance.
\end{enumerate}

These are scientific obstruction criteria, not merely software tests.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{Traceability and reading
convention}\label{traceability-and-reading-convention}

\subsection{Three coordinates, one scientific
object}\label{three-coordinates-one-scientific-object}

Every scientific node is identified by three distinct coordinates, which
must not be conflated:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Canonical node number} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NNN}}, for example \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{001}}.
  This is the stable global presentation label and follows the canonical
  derivation order.
\item
  \textbf{Semantic node ID}, for example \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}} or \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C001}}.
  This identifies the scientific owner of a definition, construction,
  theorem, control, or obstruction.
\item
  \textbf{Current section path}, for example \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.1}}. This
  records only the node's present location in the document hierarchy. It
  may change when a section is inserted or reorganized.
\end{enumerate}

The visible node label is therefore \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NNN\ ·\ ID}}, for example
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{001\ ·\ I001}}. The section path is deliberately excluded from
that label. Consequently, inserting a new section can change
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.1}} without changing either \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{001}} or \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}}.

\subsection{One DAG and its document
projections}\label{one-dag-and-its-document-projections}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/registry/dag/NODES.tsv}} and
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/registry/dag/EDGES.tsv}} are the canonical semantic
graph. The yEd Live GraphML is the canonical visual layout of the same
graph. Main-paper sections, supplementary sections, directory indices,
and code-anchor tables are generated projections; none of them
constitutes a second DAG.

For each node,
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/registry/nodes/\textless{}ID\textgreater{}.json}}
records its canonical number, semantic ID, current section path,
documentation tier, incoming and outgoing ownership, document artifacts,
and code anchors.

\subsection{Directory correspondence}\label{directory-correspondence}

The final directory component for a node has the stable form

\begin{verbatim}
NNN_ID__descriptive_slug
\end{verbatim}

For example:

\begin{verbatim}
001_I001__branching_parameter_b
\end{verbatim}

The parent directories encode the current section hierarchy. They may
change if the paper is reorganized; the final node-directory component
remains tied to \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NNN\ ·\ ID}}.

The main-paper and supplementary paths are mirrored:

\begin{verbatim}
derivation/paper/main/sections/<section parents>/NNN_ID__slug/SECTION.tex
derivation/supplement/sections/<section parents>/NNN_ID__slug/DOCUMENTATION.md
\end{verbatim}

\subsection{Relation to Python and
Lean}\label{relation-to-python-and-lean}

The semantic ID is the authoritative join key between the DAG, prose,
Python, and Lean.

Python modules use a local module-order prefix and the lower-case
semantic ID:

\begin{verbatim}
sKK_id__descriptive_slug.py
\end{verbatim}

Lean modules use the corresponding local module-order prefix and the
upper-case semantic ID:

\begin{verbatim}
SKK_ID_DescriptiveName.lean
\end{verbatim}

Here \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{KK}} is the order inside the current code section. It is not
the canonical node number \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NNN}}. A reader must therefore not
infer the scientific identity from \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{S01}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{S02}}, and so
forth alone.

The exact cross-layer lookup is provided by
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/registry/documentation/CODE\_ANCHORS.tsv}}. Each code
anchor contains:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  node number and semantic ID;
\item
  source layer and role;
\item
  exact source path;
\item
  declaration or function symbol;
\item
  declaration kind;
\item
  start and end lines;
\item
  exact source SHA-256.
\end{itemize}

The symbol plus source hash is the stable code identity. Line numbers
are a direct reading aid and must be regenerated after any source edit.

\subsection{Documentation tiers and completion
state}\label{documentation-tiers-and-completion-state}

The per-node tier \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D0}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D1}}, or \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D2}} specifies the
required depth: atomic definition, structural construction, or full
proof dossier.

A node is marked \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{COMPLETE\_V2}} only when its main-paper text,
supplementary dossier, countercheck, code anchors, artifact hashes, and
validation gates agree. Nodes are read in canonical node order.

\subsection{Reproducible lookup
procedure}\label{reproducible-lookup-procedure}

From a DAG node to the scientific text and code:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Read \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NNN\ ·\ ID}} from the node.
\item
  Locate \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ID}} in \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NODES.tsv}} or
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NODE\_TRACEABILITY\_INDEX.tsv}}.
\item
  Open the registered main-paper or supplementary artifact.
\item
  Filter \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{CODE\_ANCHORS.tsv}} by \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ID}} to obtain exact
  Python and Lean symbols, line intervals, and source hashes.
\item
  Verify the theorem or implementation status in
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/registry/nodes/\textless{}ID\textgreater{}.json}}
  and the relevant build or axiom audit.
\end{enumerate}

From a code declaration back to the DAG:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Use the semantic ID in the module name when present.
\item
  Confirm the source path and symbol in \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{CODE\_ANCHORS.tsv}}.
\item
  Follow the matching row to \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NNN\ ·\ ID}}, the current section
  path, and the node record.
\end{enumerate}

This correspondence separates scientific identity from mutable editorial
placement and from local implementation ordering. It is the basis for
all D0, D1, and D2 documentation.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{001 · I001 --- Branching parameter
b}\label{i001-branching-parameter-b}

\textbf{Canonical node label:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{001\ ·\ I001}}\\
\textbf{Semantic ID:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}}\\
\textbf{Current section path:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.1}}\\
\textbf{Documentation tier:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D0}}\\
\textbf{Node role:} free structural input\\
\textbf{Verification status:} Python tests reproduced; Lean source
kernel-built in the current prefix-free 26-core-job package.

\subsection{Position in the
derivation}\label{position-in-the-derivation}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}} is the first node in the canonical derivation order and
one of the three visible origin nodes. It has no incoming edge. Its
local responsibility is limited to the branching multiplicity that will
later parameterize the address grammar. It neither depends on the empty
carrier \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C001}} nor creates any carrier state.

This separation is intentional: a free parameter and an initial object
may coexist at the origin without one being derived from the other. The
single DAG records both as inputs to later constructions.

\subsection{Definition or statement}\label{definition-or-statement}

The scientific contract is the subtype

\[
\mathcal B = \{b\in\mathbb N \mid 2\le b\}.
\]

A CNNA finite-provenance instance supplies an explicit element
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}} of this set. The node asserts no default value and no
probability distribution over admissible values.

The lower bound is part of the declared model family. In particular, the
documentation does \textbf{not} infer that \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b\ =\ 1}} is
inconsistent in mathematics; it states only that the unary case lies
outside the admissible domain of \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}} and may be considered
separately as a comparison or control.

\subsection{Introduction reason}\label{introduction-reason}

The child-slot alphabet used by \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C003}} cannot be defined before
its cardinality is known. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}} is therefore introduced at the
origin and handed to \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C003}} through edge \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E001}}
(\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{parameterizes\_branching}}). Keeping the lower-bound proof at
this boundary prevents downstream modules from silently assuming
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b\ ≥\ 2}} as an undocumented side condition.

\subsection{Construction or encoding}\label{construction-or-encoding}

\subsubsection{Mathematical
representation}\label{mathematical-representation}

The abstract value is a natural number paired with evidence of
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{2\ ≤\ b}}.

\subsubsection{Python representation}\label{python-representation}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{BranchingParameter}} is a frozen, slotted dataclass with one
stored field, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{value}}. Construction checks:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{type(value)\ is\ int}};
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{value\ \textgreater{}=\ 2}}.
\end{enumerate}

The use of exact built-in type equality is deliberate. Python implements
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{bool}} as a subclass of \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{int}}; accepting
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{isinstance(True,\ int)}} would allow a logical flag to enter as
the numerical branching value \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1}}.

\subsubsection{Lean representation}\label{lean-representation}

Lean uses

\begin{verbatim}
structure BranchingParameter where
  value : Nat
  ge_two : 2 ≤ value
\end{verbatim}

The proposition is stored in the value itself. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{lowerBound}} is an
explicit projection theorem and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{mk\_value}} exposes the
constructor equation at the module boundary. There is no default
inhabitant and no local axiom declaration.

\subsubsection{Cross-layer
correspondence}\label{cross-layer-correspondence}

Python and Lean implement the same admissible numerical values but
enforce validity differently:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Python permits attempted invalid construction and rejects it at
  runtime;
\item
  Lean requires a proof argument before the invalid value can be
  constructed.
\end{itemize}

This is semantic equivalence of the accepted domain, not syntactic
identity of the implementations.

\subsection{Boundary case or
countercheck}\label{boundary-case-or-countercheck}

The node-local counterchecks are:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b\ =\ 2}} is admitted as the lower boundary;
\item
  larger integers are admitted without a fixed upper bound;
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b\ ∈\ \{-3,-1,0,1\}}} is rejected;
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{True}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{False}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{2.0}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{"2"}}, and
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}} are rejected by the Python type boundary;
\item
  in Lean, any claimed inhabitant automatically carries
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{2\ ≤\ value}}, so a value below two cannot be produced without
  inconsistency in the ambient logic.
\end{itemize}

These tests establish the input boundary only. They do not prove
properties of the later \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}}-ary carrier, schedule, or growth law.

\subsection{Result}\label{result}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}} closes the following narrow statement:

\begin{quote}
One explicit branching multiplicity \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}} is available, and every
accepted representation satisfies exactly \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b\ ∈\ ℕ}} and
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b\ ≥\ 2}}.
\end{quote}

No geometry, node, relation, event ordering, conductance, response, or
dynamics is introduced. The node is frozen as a verified input contract;
its documentation is now complete under schema v2.

\subsection{Downstream handoff}\label{downstream-handoff}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E001:\ I001\ →\ C003}} supplies the branching multiplicity for
  the finite slot alphabet and address grammar.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E147:\ I001\ →\ P004}} supplies the same bound to the later
  proof that the bounded canonical schedule enumerates the finite
  carrier.
\end{itemize}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E147}} is proof support, not an additional definition of
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}}.

\subsection{Code anchors}\label{code-anchors}

All line intervals refer to the current source hashes. Symbol plus
source SHA-256 is the stable identity; line numbers are reading aids.

\subsubsection{Python source}\label{python-source}

  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/code/python/src/cnna/derivation/s01\_primitive\_response\_coupled\_finite\_provenance/s01\_primitive\_inputs\_grammar\_and\_event\_order/s01\_i001\_\_branching\_parameter\_b.py}}
\item

  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{504e678397db5f6f969a5c69a6da9a2a36f34308201b0629b558331b453c1f69}}

\subsubsection{Python counterchecks}\label{python-counterchecks}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist

\section{002 · I002 --- Finite approximant depth
L}\label{i002-finite-approximant-depth-l}

\textbf{Canonical node label:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{002\ ·\ I002}}\\
\textbf{Semantic ID:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I002}}\\
\textbf{Current section path:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.2}}\\
\textbf{Documentation tier:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D0}}\\
\textbf{Node role:} free finite-cutoff input\\
\textbf{Verification status:} Python tests reproduced; Lean source
kernel-built in the current prefix-free 26-core-job package.

\subsection{Position in the
derivation}\label{position-in-the-derivation-1}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I002}} is the second canonical node and a visible origin input.
It has no incoming edge. It supplies a finite terminal depth to later
constructions but does not itself construct an address, carrier, root,
or schedule.

Its independence from \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}} is exact: branching multiplicity and
finite depth are separate coordinates of the finite approximant. Neither
determines the other.

\subsection{Definition or statement}\label{definition-or-statement-1}

The contract is

\[
L\in\mathbb N_0.
\]

Equivalently, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}} is a nonnegative integer. The value is supplied
explicitly, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L\ =\ 0}} is admissible, and no infinity,
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}}, negative value, or special unbounded sentinel belongs to
the domain.

This node does not identify \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}} with physical time, graph
distance, or a continuum coordinate. It is a finite cutoff on
provenance-address depth. The interpretation of \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L\ =\ 0}} as a
root-only approximant requires the subsequent root and grammar nodes; at
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I002}} alone it is only the lower endpoint of the input domain.

\subsection{Introduction reason}\label{introduction-reason-1}

The grammar \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C003}} must know which address depths are admitted,
and the later completion node \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C019}} must know where finite
construction terminates. Therefore \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I002}} is fixed at the origin
and transmitted through \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E002}} and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E081}}. Stating
finiteness here prevents an implicit infinity convention from entering
downstream code.

\subsection{Construction or encoding}\label{construction-or-encoding-1}

\subsubsection{Mathematical
representation}\label{mathematical-representation-1}

The abstract domain is \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ℕ₀}}; no additional predicate is required.

\subsubsection{Python representation}\label{python-representation-1}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{FiniteApproximantDepth}} is a frozen, slotted dataclass with one
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{value}} field. Its constructor checks exact built-in-integer type
and the lower bound \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{value\ \textgreater{}=\ 0}}. The value is
returned without implicit coercion by \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{to\_int}}.

\subsubsection{Lean representation}\label{lean-representation-1}

Lean uses a one-field structure:

\begin{verbatim}
structure FiniteApproximantDepth where
  value : Nat
\end{verbatim}

No proof field is necessary because \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Nat}} already enforces
nonnegativity. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{mk\_value}} records the constructor equation, and
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{zeroAdmissible}} proves the existence of an ordinary inhabitant
with value zero.

\subsubsection{Cross-layer
correspondence}\label{cross-layer-correspondence-1}

The accepted mathematical values agree exactly:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  Python built-in integers at least zero;
\item
  Lean natural numbers.
\end{itemize}

Python performs a runtime rejection of invalid external values. Lean
excludes them by type. The Python rejection of Boolean values is an
implementation-boundary refinement needed because Python's class
hierarchy differs from Lean's type separation.

\subsection{Boundary case or
countercheck}\label{boundary-case-or-countercheck-1}

The node-local checks establish:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L\ =\ 0}} is admitted and round-trips exactly;
\item
  arbitrary positive integers are admitted;
\item
  negative integers are rejected;
\item
  floating-point zero, strings, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}}, and Boolean values are
  rejected.
\end{itemize}

The countercheck distinguishes an ordinary zero-depth value from an
infinity or missing-value sentinel. No theorem in this node proves
convergence as \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L\ →\ ∞}}; finite-to-infinite completion remains a
separate downstream obligation.

\subsection{Result}\label{result-1}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I002}} closes the following statement:

\begin{quote}
One explicit finite approximant depth is available, with accepted values
exactly \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ℕ₀}}, including zero and excluding every non-finite
sentinel.
\end{quote}

The node introduces no temporal, geometric, or dynamical interpretation.
Its documentation is complete under schema v2.

\subsection{Downstream handoff}\label{downstream-handoff-1}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E002:\ I002\ →\ C003}} bounds the admitted provenance
  addresses.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E081:\ I002\ →\ C019}} sets the finite completion depth.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E148:\ I002\ →\ P004}} provides the finite cutoff to the later
  enumeration and termination proof.
\end{itemize}

These edges reuse the same input; they do not define different meanings
of \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}}.

\section{003 · C001 --- Empty carrier ∅}\label{c001-empty-carrier}

\textbf{Canonical node label:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{003\ ·\ C001}}\\
\textbf{Semantic ID:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C001}}\\
\textbf{Current section path:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.3}}\\
\textbf{Documentation tier:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D0}}\\
\textbf{Node role:} primitive initial carrier\\
\textbf{Verification status:} Python tests reproduced; Lean source
kernel-built in the current prefix-free 26-core-job package.

\subsection{Position in the
derivation}\label{position-in-the-derivation-2}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C001}} is the third canonical node and the nonparametric initial
object of the constructive chain. It appears on the same visible origin
rank as \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}} and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I002}}, but its role is different: it
is a unique state rather than a freely selected numerical input.

There is no incoming DAG edge. The lack of an incoming edge records that
the empty carrier is not generated from an earlier carrier. Its only
hard outgoing edge is the root-genesis handoff to \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}}.

\subsection{CNNA Architecture Role}\label{cnna-architecture-role}

C001 is the null baseline of \textbf{ComplemeNt Net Architecture}. It
contains no realized node, no relation, and no hidden complementary
payload. This makes the first realization at C002 an explicit
construction rather than an implicit initial condition. The statement is
architectural context, not an additional theorem.

\subsection{Definition or statement}\label{definition-or-statement-2}

Let \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{V}} be the set of present provenance nodes and
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{R\ ⊆\ V\ ×\ V}} the present directed relations. The C001 state is

\[
X_{\varnothing}=(V_{\varnothing},R_{\varnothing}),
\qquad
V_{\varnothing}=\varnothing,
\qquad
R_{\varnothing}=\varnothing.
\]

The complete local contract has three parts:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  a pre-root carrier value exists;
\item
  it contains no node;
\item
  it contains no relation.
\end{enumerate}

The node does \textbf{not} construct the root, an address, event order,
geometry, conductance, response, or hidden initialization payload.

\subsection{Introduction reason}\label{introduction-reason-2}

A derivation beginning with root genesis must distinguish the state
before genesis from the rooted state after genesis. Treating the former
as \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}}, an absent variable, or a constructorless type would
erase the source object of the transition \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C001\ →\ C002}}. C001
therefore supplies one explicit, payload-free state on which the next
construction acts.

\subsection{Construction or encoding}\label{construction-or-encoding-2}

\subsubsection{Mathematical
representation}\label{mathematical-representation-2}

The node and relation components are both empty. The representation is
unique up to equality because no local degree of freedom is present.

In the category of sets, the empty set is an initial object: for every
set \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{A}}, exactly one map \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{∅\ →\ A}} exists. This is an
exact contextual comparison for the empty component, not a claim that
the full CNNA derivation has already been formulated categorically.

\subsubsection{Python representation}\label{python-representation-2}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{EmptyCarrier}} is a frozen, slotted dataclass with no stored
fields. It exposes:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{nodes\ =\ ()}};
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{relations\ =\ ()}};
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{contains\_node(\_)\ =\ False}};
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{contains\_relation(\_,\_)\ =\ False}}.
\end{itemize}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{EMPTY\_CARRIER}} is the canonical executable value. Constructing
another zero-field instance yields an equal value, while attempting to
pass a payload raises \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{TypeError}}.

\subsubsection{Lean representation}\label{lean-representation-2}

Lean uses an inhabited singleton:

\begin{verbatim}
inductive EmptyCarrier : Type where
  | empty
\end{verbatim}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{canonical}} names the unique constructor value.
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ContainsNode}} and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ContainsRelation}} are defined as
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{False}}; \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{noNode}} and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{noRelation}} expose the
consequences. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{eqCanonical}} proves that every inhabitant is the
canonical state.

A constructorless inductive type would have no inhabitant and would
therefore encode ``no pre-root state exists,'' which is not the
contract. The one constructor represents uniqueness of the carrier
value, not one provenance node.

\subsubsection{Cross-layer
correspondence}\label{cross-layer-correspondence-2}

Python and Lean agree on the observable state:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  one canonical zero-payload carrier value exists;
\item
  every node-membership query is false;
\item
  every relation-membership query is false;
\item
  no hidden payload distinguishes alternative carriers.
\end{itemize}

The runtime and proof representations are structurally different but
semantically equivalent at the contract boundary.

\subsection{Boundary case or
countercheck}\label{boundary-case-or-countercheck-2}

The counterchecks exclude four common conflations:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Empty carrier versus missing object:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{EMPTY\_CARRIER}}
  is an actual value, not \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}}.
\item
  \textbf{Empty carrier versus rooted state:} no root is present; root
  creation belongs to \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}}.
\item
  \textbf{Empty mathematical collections versus empty datatype:} Lean's
  carrier type is inhabited even though its node and relation predicates
  are empty.
\item
  \textbf{Unique representation versus hidden parameter:} Python has no
  dataclass fields or instance dictionary, and a payload argument is
  rejected.
\end{enumerate}

The Lean theorem \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{eqCanonical}} is the exact uniqueness check. The
Python equality and field-reflection tests are executable finite checks
of the same boundary.

\subsection{Result}\label{result-2}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C001}} closes the statement:

\begin{quote}
There exists exactly one payload-free pre-root carrier representation,
and it contains neither provenance nodes nor directed relations.
\end{quote}

This result is structural only. It does not prove or assume any property
of the root that will be introduced by \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}}. The documentation
is complete under schema v2.

\subsection{Downstream handoff}\label{downstream-handoff-2}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E003:\ C001\ →\ C002}} has relation \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{root\_genesis}}. The
handoff transfers the canonical empty carrier to the root-genesis
construction. The edge note records that this is the only node birth
before any relation exists.

No other outgoing edge leaves \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C001}}; all later structures must
pass through the rooted state or other registered descendants.

\section{004 · C002 --- Root genesis r}\label{c002-root-genesis-r}

\textbf{Canonical node label:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{004\ ·\ C002}}\\
\textbf{Semantic ID:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}}\\
\textbf{Current section path:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.4}}\\
\textbf{Documentation tier:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D1}}\\
\textbf{Documentation state:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{COMPLETE\_V2}}

\subsection{Position In Derivation}\label{position-in-derivation}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}} is canonical node 004. Its only hard predecessor is
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{003\ ·\ C001}}, the unique empty carrier. The node is the first
construction that changes the cardinality of the provenance carrier:
before it, no provenance node exists; after it, exactly the root exists.

The order is essential. Neither \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I001}} nor \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{I002}} is
needed to create the root, and no address grammar is available yet.
Hence root genesis is independent of branching multiplicity and finite
cutoff. The hard incoming edge is
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E003:\ C001\ -\textgreater{}\ C002}} with relation
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{root\_genesis}}; the outgoing edge is
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E004:\ C002\ -\textgreater{}\ C003}} with relation
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{anchors\_provenance\_grammar}}.

\subsection{CNNA Architecture Role}\label{cnna-architecture-role-1}

C002 is the first realization in \textbf{ComplemeNt Net Architecture}:
the empty carrier becomes a carrier with one born root. The root has no
permanent intrinsic opposite; its continuation complement is introduced
only when C003/C018 define and order the not-yet-realized slots. This is
an interpretation of the existing genesis contract, not extra
mathematical content.

\subsection{Mathematical Contract}\label{mathematical-contract}

Let

\[
X_\varnothing=(V_\varnothing,R_\varnothing),\qquad V_\varnothing=\varnothing,\quad R_\varnothing=\varnothing
\]

be the C001 state. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}} defines one transition

\[
\Gamma_r(X_\varnothing)=X_r,
\]

such that

\[
V(X_r)=\{r\},\qquad R(X_r)=\varnothing.
\]

The complete local contract is:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  the root token \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{r}} exists;
\item
  the post-genesis carrier contains exactly \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{r}};
\item
  no ordered pair is a relation;
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{r}} has no provenance parent;
\item
  no geometric position is assigned;
\item
  no address, node ID, rank, event index, conductance, load or response
  is introduced.
\end{enumerate}

The contract is about the \textbf{immediate post-genesis state}. It does
not claim that the root remains relation-free after later births.

\subsection{Introduction Reason}\label{introduction-reason-3}

The empty carrier alone does not supply a node on which later provenance
addresses or relations can be based. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}} isolates the
irreducible birth of the root from every downstream structure. This
separation prevents the root address, first edge or first conductance
from being mistaken for primitive content of genesis.

\subsection{Explicit Construction}\label{explicit-construction}

\subsubsection{Mathematical
construction}\label{mathematical-construction}

Introduce a single zero-payload symbol \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{r}}. Define the carrier
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{X\_r}} by the singleton node set and empty relation set above.
Define parenthood and geometric-position predicates for \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{r}} to be
false at this stage.

\subsubsection{Python construction}\label{python-construction}

Python uses two frozen, slotted, zero-field dataclasses:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Root}} is the root token type;
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{RootedCarrier}} is the post-genesis carrier type.
\end{itemize}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ROOT}} and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ROOTED\_CARRIER}} are their canonical values.
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{RootedCarrier.nodes}} returns \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{(ROOT,)}},
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{relations}} returns \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{()}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{contains\_node}}
recognizes only \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ROOT}}, and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{contains\_relation}} is
constantly false. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{root\_genesis}} accepts exactly an
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{EmptyCarrier}} value and returns \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ROOTED\_CARRIER}}.

\subsubsection{Lean construction}\label{lean-construction}

Lean uses singleton inductive types \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Root}} and
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{RootedCarrier}}. Membership is represented by

\begin{verbatim}
node = Root.canonical
\end{verbatim}

and relation, parent and position predicates are definitionally
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{False}}. The transition

\begin{verbatim}
def rootGenesis (_carrier : EmptyCarrier) : RootedCarrier :=
  RootedCarrier.canonical
\end{verbatim}

is total on the C001 type and contains no branch or selected witness.

\subsection{Invariants}\label{invariants}

The construction establishes the following invariants:

\begin{longtable}[]{@{}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}
  >{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{(\columnwidth - 4\tabcolsep) * \real{0.3333}}@{}}
\toprule\noalign{}
\begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Invariant
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Formal content
\end{minipage} & \begin{minipage}[b]{\linewidth}\raggedright
Evidence
\end{minipage} \\
\midrule\noalign{}
\endhead
\bottomrule\noalign{}
\endlastfoot
Singleton node content & every admitted node equals
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Root.canonical}} & Lean \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{rootPresent}},
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{nodeUnique}}; Python \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{nodes\ ==\ (ROOT,)}} \\
Empty relation content & no source-target pair belongs & Lean
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ContainsRelation\ :=\ False}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{noRelation}}; Python
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{relations\ ==\ ()}} \\
Parentlessness & no parent value can witness parenthood of the root &
Lean \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{HasParent\ :=\ False}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{rootHasNoParent}}; Python
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{parent\_of(ROOT)\ is\ None}} \\
No geometry & no position value can witness a root position & Lean
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{HasGeometricPosition\ :=\ False}},
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{rootHasNoGeometricPosition}}; Python metadata-reflection test \\
Zero local payload & root and carrier have no data fields & Python
dataclass reflection; Lean one-constructor inductives \\
\end{longtable}

The distinction between ``one carrier value'' and ``one provenance
node'' is explicit: \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{RootedCarrier}} is the state representation;
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Root.canonical}} is the sole provenance node inside it.

\subsection{Canonicity Or Uniqueness}\label{canonicity-or-uniqueness}

Lean proves two separate uniqueness statements:

\[
\forall r':\mathrm{Root},\quad r'=r,
\]

and

\[
\forall X':\mathrm{RootedCarrier},\quad X'=X_r.
\]

These are \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Root.eqCanonical}} and
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{RootedCarrier.eqCanonical}}. Since \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{EmptyCarrier}} is
itself unique, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{rootGenesis\_eqCanonical}} shows that every
admissible source representation produces the same rooted carrier. Thus
no hidden root choice, carrier choice or genesis seed occurs at C002.

Python provides the executable analogue: zero-field dataclass instances
compare equal, canonical constants are returned, and there is no
instance dictionary in which hidden state could be stored.

\subsection{Boundary Cases}\label{boundary-cases}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{No self-edge:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{(r,r)}} is not a relation. Root birth is
  not first-relation birth.
\item
  \textbf{No parent sentinel as data:} Python returns \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}} from
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{parent\_of(ROOT)}}, but Lean states the stronger proposition
  that no parent witness exists. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}} is an API representation,
  not a mathematical parent value.
\item
  \textbf{Exact source domain:} Python rejects \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}}, tuples,
  arbitrary objects, the root token and the already rooted carrier as
  genesis inputs. Only C001's carrier type is accepted.
\item
  \textbf{No downstream metadata:} addresses, depths, ranks, event
  indices, conductances, responses and coordinates are absent.
\item
  \textbf{No dependence on \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}} or \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}}:} neither parameter
  occurs in the constructor or theorem signatures.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Result}\label{result-3}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C002}} closes the structural statement:

\begin{quote}
From the unique empty carrier there is a canonical root-genesis
transition to a unique carrier containing exactly one zero-payload root
and no relation; the root has no parent or geometry at this derivation
stage.
\end{quote}

No later structure is included in this result. The node remains frozen
under the existing core gate; the documentation update does not alter
its Python or Lean source.

\end{itemize}

At \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C003}}, the root token is anchored to the empty word. That
anchoring is a new construction and must not be read backward into C002.

\section{005 · C003 --- Finite b-ary provenance
grammar}\label{c003-finite-b-ary-provenance-grammar}

\textbf{Canonical node label:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{005\ ·\ C003}}\\
\textbf{Semantic ID:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C003}}\\
\textbf{Current section path:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.5}}\\
\textbf{Documentation tier:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D1}}\\
\textbf{Documentation state:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{COMPLETE\_V2}}

\subsection{Position In Derivation}\label{position-in-derivation-1}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C003}} is canonical node 005 and the first three-input join in
the chain. Its hard predecessors are:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{001\ ·\ I001}} through \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E001}}, supplying \(b\ge 2\);
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{002\ ·\ I002}} through \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E002}}, supplying \(L\ge 0\);
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{004\ ·\ C002}} through \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E004}}, supplying the
  already-born canonical root.
\end{itemize}

The grammar must follow root genesis because it anchors an existing root
token. It must follow both inputs because the slot alphabet depends on
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}} and finite admission depends on \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}}.

\subsection{CNNA Architecture Role}\label{cnna-architecture-role-2}

C003 supplies the first structural complement of \textbf{ComplemeNt Net
Architecture}. Realized address prefixes and admissible right-extensions
live in one bounded \(b\)-ary grammar, while the not-yet-realized
continuations form the stage-relative open complement. No second
external system is introduced.

\subsection{Mathematical Contract}\label{mathematical-contract-1}

Let

\[
S_b=\{0,1,\ldots,b-1\},\qquad b\ge2.
\]

An intrinsic provenance address is a finite word

\[
a=(a_1,\ldots,a_d)\in S_b^*,
\]

with depth \(|a|=d\). The canonical root address is the empty word
\(\varepsilon\). For \(u\in S_b^*\) and \(q\in S_b\), define right
extension

\[
\operatorname{snoc}(u,q)=u\Vert(q).
\]

The finite approximant at cutoff \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}} admits

\[
\mathcal A_{b,L}=\{a\in S_b^*:|a|\le L\}.
\]

Every nonempty address has a unique decomposition

\[
a=u\Vert(q),
\]

which defines its immediate parent \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{u}} and final slot/rank
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{q}}. The contract owns exactly:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  the local slot alphabet;
\item
  intrinsic finite words;
\item
  root anchoring to \(\varepsilon\);
\item
  child extension;
\item
  depth, parent and final-rank recovery;
\item
  finite admission by \(|a|\le L\).
\end{enumerate}

It owns no event order, node ID, geometry, metric, conductance, response
or dynamics.

\subsection{Introduction Reason}\label{introduction-reason-4}

The rooted carrier from C002 contains a root token but no language for
descendants. C003 introduces the minimal grammar needed to refer
canonically to potential descendants without importing a temporal order
or physical state. Separating the intrinsic word constructor from the
finite cutoff is essential: \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}} restricts the current approximant
but must not change the local \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}}-ary branching rule.

\subsection{Explicit Construction}\label{explicit-construction-1}

\subsubsection{Intrinsic grammar}\label{intrinsic-grammar}

The alphabet is \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{S\_b}}. The root word is empty. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{snoc}}
appends one slot at the right. The inverse operation \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{unsnoc?}}
returns \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{none}} on the root and otherwise returns the prefix word
and final slot.

The immediate parent relation is defined by

\begin{verbatim}
Parent(u,a) iff parent?(a) = some(u).
\end{verbatim}

Depth is word length. Therefore the grammar is rooted and ordered: the
final symbol records the local child slot.

\subsubsection{Finite admission}\label{finite-admission}

A bounded address is a pair consisting of an intrinsic address and a
proof that its depth is at most \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}}. The bounded child
constructor additionally requires a proof that the successor depth
remains within the cutoff.

This gives two distinct layers:

\[
S_b^* \quad\text{(intrinsic grammar)}
\]

and

\[
\mathcal A_{b,L}\subseteq S_b^* \quad\text{(finite admitted carrier)}.
\]

\subsubsection{Root anchor and predecessor
join}\label{root-anchor-and-predecessor-join}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{FiniteBAryProvenanceGrammar}} stores exactly the three
predecessor objects: the C002 rooted carrier, I001 branching parameter
and I002 cutoff. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{rootAddress}} maps the unique root token to the
bounded empty word. The \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{build\_*}} equations expose that the
canonical constructor preserves each predecessor without transformation.

These invariants show that parenthood and sibling rank are derivable
from syntax. They are not independent node labels that could disagree
with the address.

\subsection{Canonicity Or Uniqueness}\label{canonicity-or-uniqueness-1}

Three distinct canonicity claims are established:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Root anchor:} the unique C002 root maps to the empty word.
\item
  \textbf{Non-root decomposition:} a child word determines its parent
  and final slot uniquely.
\item
  \textbf{Predecessor join:} the grammar constructor contains exactly
  the supplied rooted carrier, branching parameter and cutoff.
\end{enumerate}

No enumeration order is claimed. Multiple words at the same depth are
incomparable until C018 supplies its BFS/lexicographic schedule. Hence
C003 proves canonical syntax, not canonical time.

\subsection{Boundary Cases}\label{boundary-cases-1}

\subsubsection{Zero cutoff}\label{zero-cutoff}

For \(L=0\), the admitted carrier contains only \(\varepsilon\). The
intrinsic alphabet and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{child\_address}} remain defined, but no
non-root child is admitted into the finite approximant.

\subsubsection{Root operations}\label{root-operations}

The root has no parent and no final rank. Python raises
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ValueError}}; Lean returns \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{none}}. These are equivalent
partial-operation encodings.

\subsubsection{Invalid slots and
addresses}\label{invalid-slots-and-addresses}

Ranks below zero or at least \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}} are rejected. Python
additionally rejects Boolean, floating-point, string and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{None}}
ranks, and rejects list-based addresses because the executable canonical
representation is a tuple. Lean prevents out-of-range slots by the type
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Fin\ b.value}}.

\subsubsection{Cutoff independence}\label{cutoff-independence}

The intrinsic constructor can produce a word deeper than \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}}; the
bounded grammar then rejects it. This countercheck prevents the common
but incorrect interpretation that the terminal level changes the slot
alphabet or makes child extension undefined in the unbounded grammar.

\begin{verbatim}
unsnoc?_snoc

\end{verbatim}

with \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{depth\_snoc}} and bounded \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{child\_depth}} providing
the independent depth/cutoff chain. These theorems establish the general
identities that the Python cases instantiate.

\subsection{Result}\label{result-4}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C003}} closes the statement:

\begin{quote}
Given the canonical root, branching parameter \(b\ge 2\), and finite
cutoff \(L\ge 0\), there is a canonical finite b-ary provenance grammar
whose nodes are bounded words, whose root is the empty word, and whose
non-root addresses determine unique parents and final slots.
\end{quote}

The result is grammatical and finite. It does not define a birth order,
metric, conductance or response.

\subsection{Downstream Handoff}\label{downstream-handoff-4}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E005:\ C003\ -\textgreater{}\ C004A}} supplies the grammar for
  the first provenance slot.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E063:\ C003\ -\textgreater{}\ C018}} supplies the grammar to
  the separate canonical schedule construction.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E140}} and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E149}} expose future proof obligations for
  order and finite enumeration.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E402:\ C003\ -\textgreater{}\ M050}} remains blocked because
  provenance-derived metric selection is not established by the grammar
  alone.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E260}} supports the later node-local metric-family proof gate.
\end{itemize}

The outgoing multiplicity reflects reuse of one grammar, not parallel
versions of the DAG.

\subsection{Open-provenance role: Provenance-complete
carrier}\label{open-provenance-role-provenance-complete-carrier}

C003 supplies the finite event-address grammar of the current CNNA
specialization. In the generalized open-provenance interpretation it is
the carrier on which immutable event provenance can be recorded; it does
not by itself assert that every empirical system has such a carrier.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{006 · C018 --- Canonical BFS/lexicographic provenance birth
schedule}\label{c018-canonical-bfslexicographic-provenance-birth-schedule}

\textbf{Canonical node label:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{006\ ·\ C018}}\\
\textbf{Semantic ID:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{C018}}\\
\textbf{Current section path:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.6}}\\
\textbf{Documentation tier:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D2}}\\
\textbf{Documentation state:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{COMPLETE\_V2}}

\subsection{Position In Derivation}\label{position-in-derivation-2}

C018 is canonical node 006. Its sole hard scientific predecessor is
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{005\ ·\ C003}}, which supplies the finite branching grammar, the
root address, the local slot alphabet and the depth cutoff. C018 is
introduced only after those objects exist because an order cannot be
defined before its carrier and local rank type are available.

The root itself is not scheduled: it was created by C002 and anchored to
the empty word by C003. C018 orders non-root provenance births. The
incoming hard edge is \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E063:\ C003\ -\textgreater{}\ C018}} with
relation \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{defines\_canonical\_birth\_schedule}}. The
proof-certification edge \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E141:\ P002\ -\textgreater{}\ C018}} is
non-hard and records the dedicated static-order closure. Its six public
declarations are now kernel-verified with empty axiom profiles;
least-open state selection remains owned by C004.

\subsection{Formal Statement}\label{formal-statement}

Let \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}} be the C001 branching parameter and write

\[
S_b=\{0,\ldots,b-1\},\qquad S_b^*=\bigcup_{d\ge 0}S_b^d.
\]

For local ranks \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{r,s\ in\ S\_b}}, define

\[
r <_S s \iff r<s.
\]

For provenance words \(a,c\in S_b^*\), let \(a<_{\mathrm{lex}}c\) be the
lexicographic lift of \(<_S\). C018 defines

\[
a\prec c
\iff
|a|<|c|\ \lor\ \bigl(|a|=|c|\land a<_\mathrm{lex}c\bigr).
\]

The current Lean source proves:

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{prec}} is irreflexive;
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{prec}} is transitive;
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{prec}} is asymmetric;
\item
  for all addresses \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{a,c}}, exactly one of the mutually exclusive
  cases \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{a\ prec\ c}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{a=c}}, or \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{c\ prec\ a}} holds;
\item
  every parent precedes each child;
\item
  for one parent, smaller sibling rank implies earlier child;
\item
  the relation induced on bounded slots through their child addresses is
  irreflexive, transitive and asymmetric;
\item
  two slots selecting distinct child addresses are comparable.
\end{enumerate}

The phrase \textbf{strict total order} in the C018 contract refers to
this address order and, conditionally on distinct selected children, to
the induced slot order. It does not by itself assert that a dynamically
evolving set of currently open slots has been constructed or that its
least element is unique.

\subsection{Hypotheses}\label{hypotheses}

The address-order theorems require only the C003 address type generated
from \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b}}. The lower bound \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b\ \textgreater{}=\ 2}} is
carried by \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{BranchingParameter}}, although the order-theoretic
proof itself uses only the finiteness and decidable natural values of
the local slot type.

The bounded-slot layer additionally requires:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{CanonicalBirthSchedule}} carrying a C003 grammar;
\item
  a bounded parent address;
\item
  a local rank;
\item
  a proof that the child depth satisfies
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{\textbar{}parent\textbar{}+1\ \textless{}=\ L}}.
\end{itemize}

The theorem \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{openSlotBefore\_total\_of\_distinct\_children}}
explicitly assumes that the two selected child addresses are unequal. No
theorem in C018 assumes a born-set, an event history, a response
history, or a physical state.

\subsection{Introduction Reason}\label{introduction-reason-5}

C003 constructs a rooted ordered word grammar but deliberately does not
linearize its nodes. Recurrent growth requires one deterministic choice
of which admissible slot is considered next. C018 therefore fixes a
schedule convention before C004A selects the first non-root slot and
before C004 defines the recurrent next-open-slot interface.

The convention is canonical only \textbf{relative to the locked CNNA
rule}. Other strict total orders on the same finite grammar exist. The
current node proves that the chosen rule is mathematically coherent and
unambiguous after selection; it does not derive shortlex order uniquely
from the grammar alone.

\subsection{Proof Strategy}\label{proof-strategy}

The formal proof is layered so that each mathematical responsibility is
isolated.

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Local rank order.} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{SlotBefore\ r\ s}} is defined as
  strict natural-number comparison of the finite slot values.
\item
  \textbf{Word order.} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{AddressLexBefore}} uses
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{List.Lex\ SlotBefore}}.
\item
  \textbf{Schedule order.} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{BirthBefore}} is the disjoint
  depth/lexicographic definition above.
\item
  \textbf{Same-parent compatibility.} Induction on the common parent
  word proves that appending ranks \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{r\textless{}s}} preserves
  lexicographic order.
\item
  \textbf{Word-order laws.} Structural recursion on lists proves
  lexicographic irreflexivity, transitivity and constructive trichotomy.
\item
  \textbf{Depth/lex lift.} Case analysis over the two defining branches
  and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Nat.lt\_trichotomy}} yields address-order transitivity and
  trichotomy.
\item
  \textbf{Asymmetry and totality.} Asymmetry follows by composing
  opposite directions and contradicting irreflexivity; totality for
  distinct addresses follows by eliminating the equality branch of
  trichotomy.
\item
  \textbf{Bounded slots.} A slot is mapped to its bounded C003 child
  address, and all order laws are inherited from \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{BirthBefore}}.
\end{enumerate}

This architecture avoids treating the Python loop as the proof. The
executable enumeration is evidence for the same specification, while the
Lean order laws are proved directly from the typed address grammar.

\subsection{Lemma Chain}\label{lemma-chain}

The load-bearing chain is

\begin{verbatim}
SlotBefore
  -> AddressLexBefore
  -> BirthBefore

lex_snoc_same_parent
  -> sameParentIncreasingRank

slotBefore_irrefl
  -> addressLexBefore_irrefl
  -> birthBefore_irrefl

addressLexBefore_trans
  -> birthBefore_trans
  -> birthBefore_asymm

addressLexBefore_trichotomy
  -> birthBefore_trichotomy
  -> birthBefore_total_of_ne

OpenBirthSlot.childAddress
  -> OpenSlotBefore
  -> sameParentOpenSlotIncreasingRank
  -> openSlotBefore_irrefl
  -> openSlotBefore_trans
  -> openSlotBefore_asymm
  -> openSlotBefore_total_of_distinct_children
\end{verbatim}

The proof of \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{addressLexBefore\_trans}} exhausts the constructors
of the two lexicographic witnesses. The proof of
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{addressLexBefore\_trichotomy}} compares the heads by
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Nat.lt\_trichotomy}}; equal heads reduce recursively to the
tails. \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{birthBefore\_trans}} then handles the four combinations
depth/depth, depth/lex, lex/depth and lex/lex.

\subsection{Formal Realization}\label{formal-realization}

\subsubsection{Python}\label{python}

Source:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/code/python/src/cnna/derivation/s01\_primitive\_response\_coupled\_finite\_provenance/s01\_primitive\_inputs\_grammar\_and\_event\_order/s06\_c018\_\_canonical\_bfs\_lexicographic\_provenance\_birth\_schedule.py}}\\
SHA-256:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{c99afaef142d6f3d4d74b26de21db6c1450ff1af1098f449746da5e5f6c4dae7}}

\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{OpenBirthSlot}} stores parent, rank and child explicitly.
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{open\_slot\_key}} returns \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{(len(parent),\ parent,\ rank)}}.
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{canonical\_birth\_slots}} maintains a FIFO parent list and
cursor. Whenever one parent is processed, ranks are emitted in
increasing order and each child is appended to the end of the parent
queue. Therefore all parents at depth \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{d}} are processed before
any parent at depth \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{d+1}}, and parents at one depth occur in
lexicographic order.

The key and the enumerator are extensionally consistent: comparing
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{(len(u),u,r)}} and \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{(len(v),v,s)}} is equivalent to
comparing child words \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{u\textbar{}\textbar{}(r)}} and
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{v\textbar{}\textbar{}(s)}} by depth and lexicographic order. This
statement is supported by the definitions and exhaustive finite tests in
the current package; it is not yet exported as a cross-language theorem.

\subsubsection{Lean}\label{lean}

Source:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/code/lean/core/src/CNNA/Derivation/S01\_PrimitiveResponseCoupledFiniteProvenance/S01\_PrimitiveInputsGrammarAndEventOrder/S06\_C018\_CanonicalBfsLexicographicProvenanceBirthSchedule.lean}}\\
SHA-256:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{d332704d80a9aaa1eca72b7961d868aabceeead7320bbbd5766b1d2ba2bb53a1}}

Lean represents ranks by \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Fin\ b.value}} and words by lists of
those ranks. Thus malformed local ranks cannot inhabit the slot type.
The address relation is independent of \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L}}; the cutoff enters
only when \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{OpenBirthSlot}} stores a bounded parent and a proof
that the selected child remains within the approximant. The derived slot
order compares the selected child addresses rather than comparing an
unrelated slot identifier.

\subsubsection{Executable tests}\label{executable-tests}

Source:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{derivation/code/python/tests/derivation/s01\_primitive\_response\_coupled\_finite\_provenance/s01\_primitive\_inputs\_grammar\_and\_event\_order/test\_s06\_c018\_\_canonical\_bfs\_lexicographic\_provenance\_birth\_schedule.py}}\\
SHA-256:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{12fcb32879313f7cd3e4bd9b5b004437767bd1876920aef7bf365fa5ae154653}}

The nine node-local tests verify:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  the exact binary depth-three address order;
\item
  the ternary depth-two parent/rank order;
\item
  child extension and strict adjacency direction;
\item
  irreflexivity, pairwise total direction and transitivity on the finite
  sample;
\item
  the empty non-root schedule at \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{L=0}};
\item
  determinism and absence of hidden schedule inputs;
\item
  absence of event, time, geometry, conductance, response and batch
  state;
\item
  rejection of invalid predecessors and operands;
\item
  cardinality \(\sum_{d=1}^L b^d\) and uniqueness of emitted child
  addresses for the tested ranges.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{Counterexamples Or Necessity
Checks}\label{counterexamples-or-necessity-checks}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \textbf{Depth comparison is necessary.} Pure lexicographic order may
  place a deeper word before a shallower word. For example,
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{(0,0)}} is lexicographically before \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{(1)}} under ordinary
  list lexicography, whereas the active BFS convention requires
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{(1)}} before every depth-two word.
\item
  \textbf{Sibling rank is necessary.} Omitting the final rank from the
  executable key makes all siblings of one parent indistinguishable and
  fails to determine a strict order.
\item
  \textbf{Root exclusion is necessary.} Including the empty word as a
  scheduled birth would duplicate the already completed C002 genesis and
  shift every downstream event label.
\item
  \textbf{The cutoff proof is necessary for bounded slots.} Without
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{\textbar{}parent\textbar{}+1\ \textless{}=\ L}}, the slot
  structure could select a child outside the finite approximant even
  though its parent is admitted.
\item
  \textbf{Distinct-child hypothesis is necessary for slot totality.} Two
  record values that select the same child address are not ordered in
  either direction because the underlying address relation is
  irreflexive. C018 therefore proves total comparability only after
  excluding this equality case.
\item
  \textbf{Order does not imply least-open uniqueness.} A strict total
  order on the ambient address set does not define which slots are
  currently open. A born-set predicate and proof that the relevant open
  set is nonempty are required before a least element can be selected.
  This state-dependent selection problem is deliberately outside P002
  and is owned by C004.
\item
  \textbf{Finite enumeration is not yet a Lean theorem.} Python's loop
  terminates and the tests match the carrier cardinality, but C018's
  Lean module defines the order and bounded slot objects, not an
  executable finite enumeration theorem. Exhaustivity and termination
  remain P004.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Axiom Profile}\label{axiom-profile}

C018 is part of the mathlib-free \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{cnna\_core}} package. The
current source contains no \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{axiom}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{sorry}},
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{admit}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Classical}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{noncomputable}},
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{unsafe}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{partial}}, \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{simp}}, or \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{simpa}} use.
The prefix-free source was included in the successful 26-job core build
reported for Lean 4.31.0. The node-local registry classifies the proof
audit as PASS.

The source-local theorem chain is constructive. No transitive Mathlib
choice profile is involved because this module does not import Mathlib.
The separate long-term project goal of eliminating transitive axioms in
Mathlib-dependent proof packages is therefore not a limitation of C018.

\subsection{Result}\label{result-5}

C018 establishes a coherent deterministic shortlex schedule convention:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  all shallower addresses are earlier;
\item
  addresses at one depth are lexicographically ordered;
\item
  parents precede children;
\item
  siblings follow increasing local rank;
\item
  the address order is strict and total;
\item
  bounded admissible slots inherit the order through their selected
  children.
\end{itemize}

The Python implementation enumerates the bounded non-root addresses in
that order for the tested finite grammars, and its state surface
contains no downstream physical fields.

\subsection{Remaining Limits}\label{remaining-limits}

C018 does \textbf{not} close the following statements:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  construction of the state-dependent set of currently open slots;
\item
  existence and uniqueness of its least element;
\item
  a kernel theorem equating the Python selector with the Lean relation;
\item
  finite-schedule exhaustivity and termination for every \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{b,L}};
\item
  event-index or birth-time assignment;
\item
  label-equivariance and absence of hard-coded rank bias;
\item
  response, conductance, geometry or dynamics.
\end{itemize}

These are downstream responsibilities represented by P004, C010, T003
and later nodes. P002 has closed the dedicated static-order
certification, so C018 is \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{IMPLEMENTED\_VERIFIED}}; this still
does not imply finite exhaustivity or any state-dependent least-open
theorem.

\subsection{Downstream Handoff}\label{downstream-handoff-5}

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E064\ C018\ -\textgreater{}\ C004A}}: selects the first
  provenance slot.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E065\ C018\ -\textgreater{}\ C004}}: orders the recurrent
  next-open-slot interface.
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{E066\ C018\ -\textgreater{}\ C010}}: supplies the order from
  which event indices will later be assigned; C018 itself assigns none.
\item

\subsection{Open-provenance role: Event provenance versus recurrent live
dynamics}\label{open-provenance-role-event-provenance-versus-recurrent-live-dynamics}

C018 orders birth events in an acyclic provenance history. Later
live-state feedback may be recurrent or cyclic without turning the
event-provenance relation itself into a cycle.

\begin{center}\rule{0.5\linewidth}{0.5pt}\end{center}

\section{007 · P002 --- Canonical schedule strict-total-order
closure}\label{p002-canonical-schedule-strict-total-order-closure}

\textbf{Canonical node label:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{007\ ·\ P002}}\\
\textbf{Semantic ID:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{P002}}\\
\textbf{Current section path:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{1.1.6.1}}\\
\textbf{Documentation tier:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{D2}}\\
\textbf{Documentation state:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{COMPLETE\_V2}}\\
\textbf{Proof state:} \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{KERNEL\_VERIFIED\_AXIOM\_FREE}}

\subsection{Position In Derivation}\label{position-in-derivation-3}

P002 is the proof-certification child of \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{006\ ·\ C018}}. Its hard
mathematical input is the C003 provenance-address grammar, while its
Lean proof module imports the C018 owner module in the permitted
proof-to-core direction. The DAG certification edge points from P002 to
C018; the Lean import points from \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{CNNAProofsP002}} to
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{CNNA}}.

P002 has one static responsibility:

\begin{quote}
Prove that the C018 breadth-first/lexicographic relation is a strict
total order on provenance addresses and induces a strict total order on
open-slot selected children modulo extensional equality of the selected
child address.
\end{quote}

The node does not own state-dependent least-open selection. C004 owns
least-open existence and uniqueness after C005 introduces the born
prefix and unsaturation predicate.

\subsection{Formal Statement}\label{formal-statement-1}

Let \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{BirthBefore}} be the C018 order on provenance addresses. The
public closure packages:

\textbf{EXT-REF-WORDS-001 --- established method context.} M. Lothaire,
\emph{Combinatorics on Words}, 2 ed., Cambridge Mathematical Library,
Cambridge University Press (1997). ISBN \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{9780521599245}}. DOI:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{10.1017/CBO9780511566097}}. Exact location: Ch. 1, finite words
and lexicographic order. Context: Standard finite-word lexicographic
context for the P002 static order theorem. Formal status:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{CONTEXT\_ONLY\_INTERNAL\_KERNEL\_VERIFIED}}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{¬\ BirthBefore\ a\ a}};
\item
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{BirthBefore\ a\ b\ -\textgreater{}\ BirthBefore\ b\ c\ -\textgreater{}\ BirthBefore\ a\ c}};
\item
  asymmetry;
\item
  trichotomy
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{a\ \textless{}\ b\ ∨\ a\ =\ b\ ∨\ b\ \textless{}\ a}};
\item
  comparison of distinct addresses.
\end{enumerate}

For C018 \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{OpenBirthSlot}} records it packages irreflexivity,
transitivity, and asymmetry of \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{OpenSlotBefore}}, together with
the extensional trichotomy

\[
 s <_{slot} t
 \;\lor\;
 \operatorname{child}(s)=\operatorname{child}(t)
 \;\lor\;
 t <_{slot} s.
\]

For a predicate \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{Q}} on slot records,

\[
 \operatorname{IsMinimalSelectedChild}(Q,s)
 \iff Q(s)\land\forall t\,[Q(t)\Rightarrow\neg(t<_{slot}s)].
\]

The uniqueness theorem concludes equality of the selected child
addresses of any two minimal witnesses.

\subsection{Hypotheses}\label{hypotheses-1}

The order closure quantifies only over:

\begin{itemize}
\tightlist
\item
  a C003 branching parameter and provenance addresses;
\item
  a C018 canonical schedule;
\item
  C018 open-slot records;
\item
  an arbitrary predicate on those records for the minimality theorem.
\end{itemize}

No \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{ResponseCapableState}}, born prefix, unsaturation proof,
numerical response, or Python execution is a P002 hypothesis.

\subsection{Introduction Reason}\label{introduction-reason-6}

C018 defines and proves the order primitives. P002 exposes their
reusable proof contract without enlarging C018's core API and without
importing later state layers. This gives downstream termination proofs
one named certification boundary while preserving the package direction
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{CNNAProofsP002\ -\textgreater{}\ CNNA}}.

\subsection{Proof Strategy}\label{proof-strategy-1}

\begin{enumerate}
\def\labelenumi{\arabic{enumi}.}
\tightlist
\item
  Construct the address fields directly from C018
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{birthBefore\_*}} theorems.
\item
  Construct the slot fields directly from C018
  \texttt{\protect\seqsplit{openSlotBefore\_*}} theorems.
\item
  Derive extensional slot trichotomy by applying address trichotomy to
  the two selected child addresses.
\item
  For minimal-witness uniqueness, split on equality of selected
  children.
\item
  Under inequality, use C018 total comparison of distinct selected
  children.
\item
  Each comparison direction contradicts one of the two minimality
  hypotheses.
\item
  Export the closure through a stable proposition-valued public
  contract.
\end{enumerate}

\textbf{EXT-REF-LEAN-007 --- formalization guidance.} The Lean 4
Development Team, \emph{Lean core module: Init.Data.List.Basic
(List.Lex)}, Lean FRO, LLC (2026). DOI status:
\texttt{\protect\seqsplit{NOT\_ASSIGNED\_SOFTWARE\_SOURCE}}. Stable source:
